\lesssim&\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}dP\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
\lesssim&\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},\end{split}

For the second line of (
4.8
), if
d\geq 5
we apply (
3.30
) (for
s_{1}=\frac{d-2}{2}
,
\sigma=0
) to obtain

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-1}}}\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

If
d=4
, we use and obtain (
3.23
)

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-1}}}\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

Thus,

\begin{split}&\|P\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
\end{split}

For
d=4
, this is all of (
4.8
), since the
\max
-lines are trivially zero.

So
assume from now on
d\geq 5
.
For the third line of (
4.8
) we apply (
3.30
)

\||\nabla|^{s_{1}-1}d\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s_{1}-\sigma}}}\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2-(1-\sigma)}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}.

which holds whenever

(4.9)

1-\sigma>\max\{\frac{2(s_{1}-1)}{d-2},\frac{2}{d-2}\}.

and

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{s_{2}}\left(Pd{\bf u}\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\lesssim\|P\|_{L^{\infty}}\||\nabla|^{s_{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\max_{t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{2}}\||\nabla|^{t_{1}}P\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t_{1}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{t_{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2t_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:GN:1}}{\lesssim}&\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}P\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}\\
\lesssim&\left(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}P\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\,\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}.\end{split}

which holds if we have
\sigma\in{(}\frac{2t_{2}}{d-2},1]
so in particular if

(4.10)

\sigma\in{(}\frac{2s_{2}}{d-2},1]

So we need to choose
\sigma\in[0,1]
such that (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) both hold (for a fixed
s_{1},s_{2}\in(0,\frac{d-2}{2})
with
s_{1}+s_{2}=\frac{d-2}{2}
).
That is we need to find
\sigma\in[0,1]
such that

(1-\sigma)>\max\{\frac{2(s_{1}-1)}{d-2},\frac{2}{d-2}\},\quad\sigma>\frac{2s_{2}}{d-2}

which is possible if

\begin{split}&\frac{2(s_{1}-1)}{d-2}<1-\frac{2s_{2}}{d-2}\\
\Leftrightarrow&s_{1}+s_{2}-1<\frac{d-2}{2}\\
\end{split}

(the latter one is true since
s_{1}+s_{2}=\frac{d-2}{2}
), and if

\begin{split}&\frac{2}{d-2}<1-\frac{2s_{2}}{d-2}\\
\Leftrightarrow&s_{2}<\frac{d-4}{2}\\
\end{split}

This is true unless
s_{2}=\frac{d-4}{2}
,
s_{1}=1
, that is the remaining case is

\begin{split}&\|d\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{3-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}(Pd{\bf u})\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2\frac{d-4}{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&(1+\||\nabla|^{d}P\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})\,\|d\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{3-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2\frac{d-4}{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

In this case we apply (
3.31
) and (
A.3
) for
\sigma=\frac{d-4}{d-2}
which implies

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2\frac{d-4}{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}

In summary, we have an estimate for the third line of (
4.8
) (for the right choice of
\sigma
depending on
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
)

\begin{split}&\max_{s_{1}+s_{2}=\frac{d-2}{2},s_{1},s_{2}\geq 1}\||\nabla|^{s_{1}-1}d\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s_{1}-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{s_{2}}(Pd{\bf u})\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

For the fourth line of (
4.8
) we use (
3.29
),

\||\nabla|^{s_{1}}\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s_{1}-\sigma}}}\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2-(1-\sigma)}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}

which holds if

1-\sigma>\frac{2(s_{1}-1)}{d-2}.

On the other hand, by Leibniz rule

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{s_{2}-1}d(Pd{\bf u})\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim&(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}dP\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})\,\||\nabla|^{s_{2}-1}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{split}

Recall that
s_{1}+s_{2}=\frac{d-2}{2}
and
s_{1},s_{2}\geq 1

Apply (
A.3
) to
f=|\nabla|^{-1}dd{\bf u}
then we have

\||\nabla|^{s_{2}-1}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{-1}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}

which holds if
\sigma\geq\frac{2s_{2}}{d-2}
.

So we need to find
\sigma\in[0,1]
such that

\frac{2(s_{1}-1)}{d-2}<1-\sigma\leq 1-\frac{2s_{2}}{d-2}

which again is possible since
s_{1}+s_{2}-1<\frac{d-2}{2}
.

That is, we have shown

\begin{split}&\max_{s_{1}+s_{2}=\frac{d-2}{2},s_{1},s_{2}\geq 1}\||\nabla|^{s_{1}}\Omega_{P}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s_{1}-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{s_{2}-1}d(Pd{\bf u})\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{-1}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

This proves (
4.6
) and (
4.7
) (and since the last line of (
4.8
) does not exist for (
4.7
))

In particular, we have shown for any
t\in(-T,T)

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf v}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\gamma\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

Integrating this inequality
L^{1}_{t}(-T,T)
we have

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\lesssim\gamma\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}^{2}

From Strichartz estimates
Lemma

B.1
, we have

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf v}\|_{C^{0}_{t}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}{d}{\bf v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}^{2}.\end{split}

We can conclude.
∎

We need to obtain similar estimates for
W=Pd{\bf u}-d{\bf v}
, which for
\alpha\in[0,\ldots,d]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
we write as

W_{i}^{\alpha}:=P_{ij}\partial_{\alpha}u^{j}-\partial_{\alpha}v^{i}.

As usual we identify
\partial_{0}
with
\partial_{t}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

We have

(4.11)

\begin{split}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf W}^{0}=&-\sum_{a=1}^{d}\partial_{a}(-\Omega_{P}^{a}\,P\partial_{t}{\bf u}-\Omega_{P}^{0}\,P\partial_{a}{\bf u}+P\,G_{2;a,0}({\bf u})).\\
\end{split}

and for
b\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,

(4.12)

\begin{split}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf W}^{b}=&\partial_{t}(\Omega_{P}^{0}\,P\partial_{b}{\bf u}+\Omega_{P}^{b}\,P\partial_{t}{\bf u}-P\,G_{2;b,0}({\bf u}))\\
&-\sum_{a=1}^{d}\partial_{a}(-\Omega_{P}^{a}\,P\partial_{b}{\bf u}+\Omega_{P}^{b}\,P\partial_{a}{\bf u}+P\,G_{2;a,b}({\bf u})).\\
\end{split}

where we chose “
+
” for
\pm
if
\beta=0
, and
-
for
\pm
if
\beta\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
.

Proof.

Essentially by construction, we have for any
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}

\begin{split}\partial_{t}W_{i}^{0}-\sum_{a=1}^{d}\partial_{a}W_{i}^{a}=&\partial_{t}(P_{ij}\partial_{t}u^{j}-\partial_{t}v^{i})-\partial_{a}(P_{ij}\partial_{a}u^{j}-\partial_{a}v^{i})\\
=&\partial_{t}(P_{ij}\partial_{t}u^{j})-\operatorname{div}(P_{ij}\nabla u^{j})-\left(\partial_{tt}v^{i}-\Delta v^{i}\right)=0.\end{split}

We then have

\begin{split}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)W_{i}^{\beta}=&\partial_{\beta}(\underbrace{\partial_{t}W_{i}^{t}-\partial_{a}W_{i}^{a}}_{=0})+\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}W_{i}^{\beta}-\partial_{\beta}W_{i}^{t})-\partial_{a}(\partial_{a}W_{i}^{\beta}-\partial_{\beta}W_{i}^{a})\\
=&\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}(P_{ij}\partial_{\beta}u^{j})-\partial_{\beta}(P_{ij}\partial_{t}u^{j}))-\partial_{a}(\partial_{a}(P_{ij}\partial_{\beta}u^{j})-\partial_{\beta}(P_{ij}\partial_{a}u^{j}))\\
=&\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}P_{ij}\,\partial_{\beta}u^{j}-\partial_{\beta}P_{ij}\,\partial_{t}u^{j})-\partial_{a}(\partial_{a}P_{ij}\partial_{\beta}u^{j}-\partial_{\beta}P_{ij}\,\partial_{a}u^{j}).\\
\end{split}

We now need to distinguish between
\beta=0
and
\beta\neq 0
.

For
\beta=0
we have

\begin{split}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)W_{i}^{0}=&-\sum_{a=1}^{d}\partial_{a}(\partial_{a}P_{ij}\partial_{t}u^{j}-\partial_{t}P_{ij}\,\partial_{a}u^{j}).\\
\end{split}

We now use the cancellation condition (
2.20
).

\begin{split}&\partial_{a}P\partial_{t}{\bf u}-\partial_{t}P\,\partial_{a}{\bf u}\\
=&\left(\partial_{a}P\,P^{T}-P\Omega^{a}P^{T}\right)P\partial_{t}{\bf u}-\left(\partial_{t}P\,P^{T}+P\Omega^{0}P^{T}\right)P\partial_{a}{\bf u}\\
&+P\left(\Omega^{a}\partial_{t}{\bf u}+\Omega^{0}\partial_{a}{\bf u}\right)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:OmegaPalpha},\eqref{eq:omeacommdu}}{=}&-\Omega_{P}^{a}\,P\partial_{t}{\bf u}-\Omega_{P}^{0}\,P\partial_{a}{\bf u}+P\,G_{2;a,0}({\bf u})\\
\end{split}

That is, we have established (
4.11
).

For
\beta=b\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
we observe

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}P\,\partial_{b}{\bf u}-\partial_{b}P\,\partial_{t}{\bf u}\\
=&\left(\partial_{t}P\,P^{T}+P\Omega^{0}P^{T}\right)\,P\partial_{b}{\bf u}+\left(-\partial_{b}P\,P^{T}+P\Omega^{b}P^{T}\right)P\partial_{t}{\bf u}\\
&-P\left(\Omega^{0}\partial_{b}{\bf u}+\Omega^{b}\partial_{t}{\bf u}\right)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:OmegaPalpha},\eqref{eq:omeacommdu}}{=}&\Omega_{P}^{0}\,P\partial_{b}{\bf u}+\Omega_{P}^{b}\,P\partial_{t}{\bf u}-P\,G_{2;b,0}({\bf u}).\end{split}

Also

\begin{split}&\partial_{a}P\,\partial_{b}{\bf u}-\partial_{b}P\,\partial_{a}{\bf u}\\
=&\left(\partial_{a}P\,P^{T}-P\Omega^{a}P^{T}\right)P\partial_{b}{\bf u}-\left(\partial_{b}P\,P^{T}-P\Omega^{b}P^{T}\right)P\partial_{a}{\bf u}\\
&+P\left(\Omega^{a}\partial_{b}{\bf u}-\Omega^{b}\partial_{a}{\bf u}\right)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:OmegaPalpha},\eqref{eq:omeacommdu}}{=}&-\Omega_{P}^{a}\,P\partial_{b}{\bf u}+\Omega_{P}^{b}\,P\partial_{a}{\bf u}+P\,G_{2;a,b}({\bf u})\\
\end{split}

Thus, we have (
4.12
).

We can conclude
∎

We also have control of the initial data of
W
:

Lemma 4.3
.

For
\varepsilon
in (
2.10
) small enough we have

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf W}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\partial_{t}{\bf W}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.

We observe that by the choice of
{\bf v}(0)=\partial_{t}{\bf v}(0)=0
we have,

W_{i}^{a}\Big|_{t=0}=P_{ij}\partial_{a}{\bf u_{0}},\quad W_{i}^{0}\Big|_{t=0}=P_{ij}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}

Then in view of
Lemma

3.4
and (
2.10
)

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim&\max_{s\in[0,\frac{d-2}{2}]}\||\nabla|^{s}P\|_{L^{\frac{d}{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}-s}\nabla{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}-s}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
\lesssim&\left(\|P\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}P\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

Next, we have

\partial_{t}W^{a}\Big|_{t=0}=\partial_{t}(P\partial_{a}{\bf u})\Big|_{t=0}=\partial_{t}P\partial_{a}{\bf u}\Big|_{t=0}+P\partial_{a}\partial_{t}{\bf u}\Big|_{t=0}.

Consequently,

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\partial_{t}W^{a}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\left(\partial_{t}P\,\partial_{a}{\bf u_{0}}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\left(P\,\partial_{a}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{s\in[0,\frac{d-4}{2}]}\left(\||\nabla|^{s}dP\|_{L^{\frac{d}{s+1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}-s}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(s+1)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\||\nabla|^{s}P\|_{L^{\frac{d}{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}-s}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
\lesssim&\left(\|P\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}dP\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right).\\
\end{split}

So again in view of
Lemma

3.4
and (
2.10
),

\max_{a\in\{1,\ldots,d\}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\partial_{t}W^{a}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Lastly, we observe that from the definition of
{\bf v}
, i.e. (
4.2
) and (
4.1
),

\left(\partial_{tt}-\Delta\right){\bf v}=\partial_{t}(P\partial_{t}{\bf u})-\operatorname{div}(P\nabla{\bf u}),

that is

\partial_{t}(P\partial_{t}{\bf u})-\partial_{tt}{\bf v}=\operatorname{div}(P\nabla{\bf u})-\Delta{\bf v}.

Consequently,

\begin{split}\partial_{t}W^{0}=&\partial_{t}(P\partial_{t}{\bf u})-\partial_{tt}{\bf v}\\
=&\operatorname{div}(P\nabla{\bf u})-\Delta{\bf v}.\end{split}

Since
{\bf v}(0)=0
we conclude

\partial_{t}W^{0}\Big|_{t=0}=\operatorname{div}(P\nabla{\bf u})\Big|_{t=0}=\nabla P\Big|_{t=0}\cdot\nabla{\bf u_{0}}+P\Big|_{t=0}\Delta{\bf u_{0}}.

But then

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}W^{0}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{s\in[0,\ldots,\frac{d-4}{2}]}\left(\||\nabla|^{1+s}P\|_{L^{\frac{d}{1+s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}-s}{\bf u}_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(1+s)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{s}P\|_{L^{\frac{d}{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-s}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
\lesssim&\left(\|P\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}P\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

In the last step we used again
Lemma

3.4
and (
2.10
).

We can conclude.
∎

As a consequence of
Lemma

4.2
and
Lemma

4.3
we obtain

Lemma 4.4

(Estimates of
W
)
.

Let
s\in(1,\frac{d^{2}-4d+1}{2(d-1)})
. There exists
\varepsilon>0
such that if (
2.10
) is satisfied with that
\varepsilon
,

(4.13)

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W\|_{C^{0}_{t}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&+\||\nabla|^{s-1}W\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times(0,T)}\\
\lesssim_{s}&\||\nabla|^{s-2}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}^{2}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

Proof.

From Strichartz estimates,
Lemma

B.1
, taking into account also
Lemma

4.3
,

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}dW\|_{C^{0}_{t}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}W\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times(0,T)}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\partial_{t}W(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\left((\partial_{tt}-\Delta)W)\right)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\left((\partial_{tt}-\Delta)W)\right)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}.\end{split}

In view of
Lemma

4.2
we have for fixed time
t\in(-T,T)

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf W}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{\alpha,\beta\in\{0,\ldots,d\}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left(\Omega_{P}^{\alpha}\,P^{T}\partial_{\beta}{\bf u}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\max_{\alpha,\beta\in\{0,\ldots,d\}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d\left(PG_{2;\alpha,\beta}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\partial_{t}(\Omega_{P}^{0}\,P\partial_{b}{\bf u})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}\partial_{t}(\Omega_{P}^{b}\,P\partial_{t}{\bf u})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:adasd2}}{\lesssim}&\||\nabla|^{s-2}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
&+\max_{\alpha,\beta\in\{0,\ldots,d\}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d\left(PG_{2;\alpha,\beta}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

As before when obtaining the estimate (
4.5
) we observe that by
Lemma

3.4
,

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d\left(PG_{2;\alpha,\beta}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\left(\|P\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}dP\|_{L^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}dG_{2;\alpha,\beta}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:G2cond:1},\eqref{eq:411Ssmall}}{\lesssim}&\gamma\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
\end{split}

Consequently, we have shown that for each fixed
t\in(-T,T)
we have

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf W}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\gamma\||\nabla|^{s-2}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

Integrating this in time we find (
4.13
). We can conclude.
∎

Combining
Lemma

4.4
,
Lemma

4.1
we have

Proof of
Theorem

1.1
.

From (
4.3
)

d{\bf u}=P^{T}{\bf W}+P^{t}d{\bf v},

Thus for each fixed time
t\in(-T,T)

(4.14)

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim&\left(\|P\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}P\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf W}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf v}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf W}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf v}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\end{split}

where in the last step we used
Lemma

3.4
and (
2.10
).

Similarly,

(4.15)

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim&\left(\|P\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}P\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\left(\||\nabla|^{s-1}{\bf W}\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf v}\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{s-1}{\bf W}\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf v}\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

Combining (
4.14
), (
4.15
) with (
4.4
) and (
4.13
) we obtain

(4.16)

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}^{2}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{split}

In particular, if we set

A(\tau):=\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-\tau,\tau))}

then
\tau\mapsto A(\tau)
is continuous (by absolute continuity of the integral), and we have for any
\tau\leq T

A(\tau)\leq C\,A(\tau)^{2}+C\,B,

where

B:=\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right).

If
B<\frac{1}{4C^{2}}
then we conclude from the polynomial inequality above

\text{either }A(\tau)\leq\frac{1}{2C}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4C^{2}}-B}\quad\text{or}\quad A(\tau)\geq\frac{1}{2C}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4C^{2}}-B}

Observe that the first option is equivalent to

\begin{split}&\sqrt{\frac{1}{4C^{2}}-B}\leq\frac{1}{2C}-A(\tau)\\
\Leftrightarrow&\frac{1}{4C^{2}}-B\leq\frac{1}{4C^{2}}+\left(A(\tau)\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{C}A(\tau)\\
\Leftrightarrow&\frac{1}{C}A(\tau)\leq B+\left(A(\tau)\right)^{2}\\
\end{split}

Clearly for
\tau=0
this is satisfied (since
C\geq 1
). Since
\tau\mapsto A(\tau)
is continuous, we find that

A(\tau)\leq\frac{1}{2C}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4C^{2}}-B}\quad\forall\tau\in(0,T]

and in particular we have

A(T)\lesssim B.

Thus, (
4.16
) becomes

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\\
\lesssim&(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{2}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf\dot{u}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

That is, we have established (
1.4
) and can conclude.
∎

5.
Halfwave map and wave map equation fit into Theorem
1.1

5.1.
Wave map equation

Firstly, we observe that our assumptions are applicable to the classical wave map equation

\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}\perp T_{{\bf u}}{\mathbb{S}}^{2}.

That is

\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}={\bf u}\langle{\bf u},\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}\rangle

Set

\Omega^{\alpha}_{ij}:=\begin{cases}u^{i}\partial_{a}u^{j}-u^{j}\partial_{a}u^{i}\quad&a=\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\\
-\left(u^{i}\partial_{t}u^{j}-u^{j}\partial_{t}u^{i}\right)\quad&\alpha=0\\
\end{cases}

Then we observe that

\begin{split}&u^{i}\langle{\bf u},\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}\rangle\\
=&-u^{i}\left(\langle\partial_{t}{\bf u},\partial_{t}{\bf u}\rangle-\sum_{a=1}^{d}\langle\partial_{a}{\bf u},\partial_{a}{\bf u}\rangle\right)\\
=&-u^{i}\left(\partial_{t}u^{j}\,\partial_{t}u^{j}-\sum_{a=1}^{d}\partial_{a}u^{j}\,\partial_{a}u^{j}\right)\\
=&-\left(u^{i}\partial_{t}u^{j}\,-u^{j}\partial_{t}u^{i}\right)\partial_{t}u^{j}\\
&+\sum_{a=1}^{d}\left(u^{i}\partial_{a}u^{j}-u^{j}\partial_{a}u^{i}\right)\partial_{a}u^{j}\\
=&\left(\Omega\cdot d{\bf u}\right)_{i}\end{split}

So we can choose
F\equiv 0
in (
1.2
), which is clearly admissible in the sense of
Definition

2.2
.

As for
\Omega
we now show

Proposition 5.1
.

\Omega
as above for the wave maps into
{\mathbb{S}}^{2}
satisfies the conditions of
Definition

2.4
.

Indeed, it is clearly antisymmetric,
\Omega_{ij}^{\alpha}=-\Omega_{ji}^{\alpha}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

\Omega
satisfies the growth conditions (
2.1
), (
2.2
)

Proof.

We have for
p<\frac{d}{s+1}

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{s}d\Omega\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{s}({\bf u}\,dd{\bf u})\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{s}(d{\bf u}\,d{\bf u})\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{t\in[0,s]}\||\nabla|^{t}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{s-t}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{(\frac{dp}{d-tp},q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{t}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t+1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{s-t}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{dp}{d-(t+1)p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\left(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\||\nabla|^{s}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\left(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\left(\||\nabla|^{s+1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{s}\partial_{tt}{\bf u}\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
\end{split}

Similar to the proof of
Lemma

2.3
, we observe

\partial_{tt}{\bf u}=\Delta{\bf u}+{\bf u}|d{\bf u}|^{2}

So,

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{s}\partial_{tt}{\bf u}\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{s+1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{s}({\bf u}|d{\bf u}|^{2})\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{s+1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\max_{t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}=s,\ t_{i}\geq 0}\||\nabla|^{t_{1}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t_{1}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{t_{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t_{2}+1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{t_{3}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(\frac{dp}{d-(t_{1}+t_{2}+1)p},q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2})\||\nabla|^{s+1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

We conclude that

\||\nabla|^{s}d\Omega\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{3})\,\||\nabla|^{s+1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

which establishes (
2.1
).

The proof of (
2.2
) is almost verbatim only without the
\partial_{tt}
term (and essentially for
s-1
instead of
s
, leading to the assumption
p<\frac{d}{s}
in this case).
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

\Omega
satisfies the curl-conditions (
2.13
), (
2.14
), (
2.15
), (
2.16
), (
2.17
),(
2.18
) (
2.19
).

Proof.

We observe

\begin{split}\partial_{a}\Omega^{b}-\partial_{b}\Omega^{a}=&\partial_{a}u^{i}\,\partial_{b}u^{j}-\partial_{b}u^{i}\,\partial_{a}u^{j}-\left(\partial_{a}u^{j}\,\partial_{b}u^{i}-\partial_{b}u^{j}\,\partial_{a}u^{i}\right)\\
=&2\partial_{a}u^{i}\,\partial_{b}u^{j}-2\partial_{b}u^{i}\,\partial_{a}u^{j},\end{split}

and similarly

\begin{split}\partial_{a}\Omega^{0}+\partial_{t}\Omega^{a}=&-\partial_{a}(u^{i}\partial_{t}u^{j}-u^{j}\partial_{t}u^{i})+\partial_{t}(u^{i}\partial_{a}u^{j}-u^{j}\partial_{a}u^{i})\\
=&-\partial_{a}u^{i}\partial_{t}u^{j}+\partial_{a}u^{j}\partial_{t}u^{i}+\partial_{t}u^{i}\partial_{a}u^{j}-\partial_{t}u^{j}\partial_{a}u^{i}\\
=&-2\partial_{a}u^{i}\partial_{t}u^{j}+2\partial_{a}u^{j}\partial_{t}u^{i}\\
\end{split}

So elements of
G_{1;\alpha,\beta}({\bf u})
are of the form
\partial_{\alpha}u^{i}\,\partial_{\beta}u^{j}
.

As for (
2.13
)
we observe for
1\leq\tilde{s}\leq d
,
1<p<\frac{2d}{\tilde{s}+2}

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\tilde{s}}\left(\partial_{\alpha}u\,\partial_{\beta}v\right)\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{t\in[0,\frac{\tilde{s}}{2}]}\||\nabla|^{t}\partial_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t+1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\tilde{s}-t}\partial_{\beta}v\|_{L^{(\frac{dp}{d-(t+1)p},q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\max_{t\in[\frac{\tilde{s}}{2},\tilde{s}]}\||\nabla|^{t}\partial_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{(\frac{dp}{d-(1+\tilde{s}-t)p,q)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\tilde{s}-t}\partial_{\beta}v\|_{L^{\frac{d}{1+\tilde{s}-t}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}du\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\tilde{s}+1}dv\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\tilde{s}+1}du\|_{L^{(p,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}dv\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

This readily establishes (
2.13
) for
s:=\tilde{s}+1
,

As for (
2.14
),
since
d\geq 4
we have from Sobolev embedding,

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-6}{2}}d(\partial_{\alpha}u\ \partial_{\beta}v)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d(\partial_{\alpha}u\ \partial_{\beta}v)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{t\in[0,\frac{d-4}{2}]}\||\nabla|^{t}ddu\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{4+2t}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}-t}dv\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2-2t}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\max_{t\in[0,\frac{d-4}{2}]}\||\nabla|^{t}du\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2+2t}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}-t}ddv\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2t}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}ddu\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}ddv\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\,\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}du\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}dv\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\end{split}

As for (
2.15
) and (
2.16
),
by Hölder’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\|d\left(\partial_{\alpha}u\ \partial_{\beta}v\right)\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{5-\sigma},q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim\|ddu\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{4-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|dv\|_{L^{(2d,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|ddv\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{4-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|du\|_{L^{(2d,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

This readily proves (
2.15
) by taking
\sigma=0
. For (
2.16
), apply (
A.3
), whenever
(1-\sigma)\in[\frac{2}{d-2},1]
,

(5.1)

\|dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{4-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\||\nabla||\nabla|^{-1}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{4-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{-1}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}.

Thus

\begin{split}&\|dG_{1;\alpha,\beta}({\bf u})\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{5-\sigma},q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{-1}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\,\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,q)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{split}

As for (
2.17
),
similar to the previous argument

\|\partial_{\alpha}u\ \partial_{\beta}v\|_{L^{(d,1)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|du\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|dv\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

As for (
2.18
),
it is a direct consequence of (
A.5
).

As for (
2.19
),
assume
(1-\sigma)\in[\frac{2(s-1)}{d-2},1]
and
s\in[2,\frac{d-2}{2}]
.

If
s=2
we observe

\begin{split}&\|\partial_{\alpha}du\ \partial_{\beta}v\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{5-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|\partial_{\alpha}du\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{4-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\partial_{\beta}v\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:wm:askldalskjd}}{\lesssim}&\||\nabla|^{-1}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}dd{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\|dv\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

This proves the claim for
s=2
and
1-\sigma\in[\frac{2}{d-2},1]
.

If
s>2

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{s-2}\left(\partial_{\alpha}du\ \partial_{\beta}v\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s-\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{t\in[0,s-2]}\||\nabla|^{t+1}|\nabla|^{-1}ddu\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2(1+t)+\sigma_{1}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{s-2-t}dv\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2(s-2-t)+\sigma_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:GN:1}}{\lesssim}&\max_{t\in[0,s-2]}\||\nabla|^{-1}ddu\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{1}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}ddu\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{1}}\|dv\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{2}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}dv\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{2}}\end{split}

for
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}=1-\sigma
, where we must ensure
\sigma_{1}\in[\frac{2(t+1)}{d-2},1]
,
\sigma_{2}\in[\frac{2(s-2-t)}{d-2},1]
which is possible since
(1-\sigma)\in[\frac{2(s-1)}{d-2},1]
. We can conclude.
∎

Lastly, we observe

\begin{split}&\left(\Omega^{a}\partial_{b}{\bf u}-\Omega^{b}\partial_{a}{\bf u}\right)_{i}\\
=&u^{i}\partial_{a}u^{j}\partial_{b}u^{j}-\underbrace{u^{j}\partial_{a}u^{i}\partial_{b}u^{j}}_{=0}-u^{i}\partial_{b}u^{j}\partial_{a}u^{j}\underbrace{+u^{j}\partial_{b}u^{i}\partial_{a}u^{j}}_{=0}\\
=&u^{i}\partial_{a}u^{j}\partial_{b}u^{j}-u^{i}\partial_{b}u^{j}\partial_{a}u^{j}\\
=&0\end{split}

and similarly

\begin{split}&\left(\Omega^{a}\partial_{t}{\bf u}+\Omega^{0}\partial_{a}{\bf u}\right)_{i}\\
=&u^{i}\partial_{a}u^{j}\partial_{t}u^{j}-\underbrace{u^{j}\partial_{a}u^{i}}_{=0}\,\partial_{t}u^{j}-u^{i}\partial_{t}u^{j}\,\partial_{a}u^{j}+\underbrace{u^{j}\partial_{t}u^{i}\,\partial_{a}u^{j}}_{=0}\\
=&0\end{split}

That is the cancellation condition (
2.20
), (
2.21
) is trivially satisfied.

That is,
Proposition

5.1
is established.

5.2.
Halfwave map equation

The other application is the halfwave map equation for
{\bf u}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[-T,T]\to{\mathbb{S}}^{2}

\partial_{t}{\bf u}-{\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u}=0.

Krieger and Sire showed in
[
13
]
that we can solve instead

(5.2)

\begin{split}\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}=&{\bf u}|\nabla{\bf u}|^{2}-{\bf u}||\nabla|{}{\bf u}|^{2}\\
&+|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\,\left(\left\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right\rangle\right)\\
&+{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right).\end{split}

We can write

\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}={\bf u}\langle{\bf u},\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}\rangle+\Pi_{T_{u}{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\left(\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}\right),

As discussed above for the wave-map equation we can choose
\Omega
admissible so that

{\bf u}\langle{\bf u},\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}\rangle=\Omega\cdot d{\bf u}.

So we set
\Pi_{T_{u}{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}{\bf v}={\bf v}-{\bf u}\langle{\bf u},{\bf v}\rangle
and thus we set

\begin{split}F({\bf u}):=&\Pi_{T_{u}{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\left(\partial_{tt}{\bf u}-\Delta{\bf u}\right)\\
=&|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\,\left\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right\rangle-{\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}\\
&+{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right)\end{split}

where we got rid of time-derivatives
\partial_{t}{\bf u}
by the half-wave map equation itself.

We need to prove
F
is admissible in the sense of
Definition

2.2
, i.e. we need to prove the growth estimate (
2.5
) and the perturbative estimate (
2.7
).

We look at the terms of
F({\bf u})
one by one.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
{\bf u}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to{\mathbb{S}}^{2}
,
then we have the corresponding growth estimate for (
2.5
), for any
p<\frac{d}{t+1}

\||\nabla|^{t}\left(|\nabla|{\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\right)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\||\nabla|^{t+2}{\bf u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

the corresponding estimate to (
2.6
)

\||\nabla|{\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{3},2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\

and the corresponding perturbative estimate for (
2.7
)

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left(|\nabla|{\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\right)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

Proof.

For the growth estimate corresponding to (
2.5
) we observe

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{t}\left(|\nabla|{\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\right)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{p<\frac{d}{t+1}}{\lesssim}&\max_{t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}=t}\||\nabla|^{1+t_{1}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{dp}{d-(t+1-t_{1})p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{1/2+t_{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2t_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{1/2+t_{3}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2t_{3}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{t+2}{\bf u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

If
t=0
the same argument implies

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{t}\left(|\nabla|{\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\right)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{dp}{d-p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
\end{split}

which for
p=\frac{2}{3}d
implies the second claim corresponding to (
2.6
).

The second perturbative estimate follows from (
A.6
), observing that

\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle=H_{|\nabla|}\left(|\nabla|^{-1}|\nabla|{\bf u}\cdot,|\nabla|^{-1}|\nabla|{\bf u})\right).

∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
{\bf u}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to{\mathbb{S}}^{2}
, then we have the corresponding growth estimate for (
2.5
), for any
p<\frac{d}{t+1}

\||\nabla|^{t}\left({\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}\right)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\right)\||\nabla|^{t+2}{\bf u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

the corresponding estimate to (
2.6
)

\|\left({\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}\right)\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{3},2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\

and the corresponding perturbative estimate for (
2.7
)

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left({\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}\right)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

Proof.

For the growth estimate, by writing

{\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}={\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle H_{|\nabla|}({\bf u}\cdot,{\bf u}),

we observe

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{t}\left({\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}\right)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{\sum_{i=1}^{5}t_{i}=t}\||\nabla|^{t_{1}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t_{1}}}}\||\nabla|^{t_{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{1+t_{3}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{dp}{d-(t+1-t_{3})p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{\frac{1}{2}+t_{4}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+t_{4}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{1}{2}+t_{5}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+t_{5}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\left(\|{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\|{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{3}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\right)\,\||\nabla|^{t+2}{\bf u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

The corresponding estimate to (
2.6
) follows as in the proof of
Lemma

5.4
since

\left\lvert{\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}\right\rvert\lesssim\left\lvert|\nabla|{\bf u}\right\rvert\,\left\lvert\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\right\rvert.

For the perturbative estimate we write

{\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}={\bf u}\,u^{i}|\nabla|u^{i}\,H_{|\nabla|}\left(|\nabla|^{-1}|\nabla|{\bf u}\cdot,|\nabla|^{-1}|\nabla|{\bf u})\right)

Set
Q={\bf u}{\bf u}^{i}
. We observe that

\begin{split}\||\nabla|Q\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim&\|{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{1}{4}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{4d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{3}{4}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{(4d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

and

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}|\nabla|Q\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\|{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Consequently, from (
A.6
)

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left({\bf u}\,|\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle|^{2}\right)\|_{L^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\left(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\right)\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\end{split}

∎

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
{\bf u}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to{\mathbb{S}}^{2}
, then we have the corresponding growth estimate for (
2.5
): for
t\in[1,\frac{2d-1}{4}]
,
p\in(1,\frac{2d}{2t+\frac{1}{2}})
,

\||\nabla|^{t}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right)\right)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{4}\right)\||\nabla|^{t+2}{\bf u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

the corresponding estimate to (
2.6
)

\|\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right)\right)\|_{L^{(\frac{2d}{3},2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\

and the corresponding perturbative estimate for (
2.7
)

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right)\right)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left(1+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right)\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

Proof.

We have

(5.3)

\begin{split}&{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right)\\
=&{\bf u}\wedge H_{|\nabla|}({\bf u}\wedge,|\nabla|{\bf u})\\
\end{split}

This is enough to obtain the growth estimate corresponding to (
2.5
) via the Leibniz rule:

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{t}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right)\right)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}=t}\||\nabla|^{t_{1}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{d}{t_{1}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{1}{4}+t_{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{4d}{1+4t_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{7}{4}+t_{3}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{dp}{d-(t+\frac{1}{4}-t_{3})p}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\left(\|{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{t+2}{\bf u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

This makes sense whenever
d-(t+\frac{1}{4})p>0
, i.e.
p<\frac{2d}{2t+\frac{1}{2}}
and
\frac{1}{4}+t\leq\frac{d}{2}
.
Thus the first growth estimate, corresponding to (
2.5
), is established.

For the second growth estimate, corresponding to (
2.6
) we observe that for very small
\alpha>0

\begin{split}&\|{\bf u}\wedge H_{|\nabla|}({\bf u}\wedge,|\nabla|{\bf u})\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{3}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{1-\alpha}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1-2\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{1+\alpha}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2+2\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

We still have to establish the perturbative estimate corresponding to (
2.7
). Since

{\bf a}\wedge({\bf b}\wedge{\bf c})={\bf b}({\bf c}\cdot{\bf a})-{\bf c}({\bf a}\cdot{\bf b}),

we have

\begin{split}&{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right)\\
=&\left({\bf u}\wedge H_{|\nabla|}({\bf u}\wedge,|\nabla|{\bf u})\right)^{i}\\
=&\sum_{j=1}^{3}u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{i},|\nabla|u^{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{3}u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},|\nabla|u^{i})\\
\end{split}

Now observe that since
d
is an integer and
d\geq 4
we have

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\approx\||\nabla|^{t}\nabla^{k}F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

where
k=\lfloor\frac{d-2}{2}\rfloor
and
t=\frac{d-2}{2}-\lfloor\frac{d-2}{2}\rfloor\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}
.

We then have

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left(u^{i}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},|\nabla|u^{k})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}=k}\||\nabla|^{t}\left(D^{s_{1}}u^{i}H_{|\nabla|}(D^{s_{2}}u^{j},D^{s_{3}}|\nabla|u^{k})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{split}

here
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}
are integers.

We first observe that if
s_{1}\geq 1
then we can apply (
A.3
), and for for
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}=1
,

\begin{split}&\max_{s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}=s,s_{1}\geq 1}\||\nabla|^{t}\left(D^{s_{1}}u^{i}H_{|\nabla|}(D^{s_{2}}u^{j},D^{s_{3}}|\nabla|u^{k})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}=\frac{d-2}{2},\,t_{1}\geq 3/4}\||\nabla|^{t_{1}}u^{i}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2(t_{1}-1)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{\frac{1}{2}+t_{2}}u^{j}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2(t_{2}-1/2)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{\frac{3}{2}+t_{3}}u^{k}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{3}+2(1/2+t_{3})}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|u^{i}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{1}}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{1}}\,\||\nabla|u^{j}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{2}}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{2}}\,\||\nabla|u^{k}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{3}}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{3}}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}}^{2}.\end{split}

For this to work we need
\sigma_{1}\geq\frac{\min\{0,2(t_{1}-1)\}}{d-2}
,
\sigma_{2}\geq\frac{\min\{0,2(1/2+t_{2}-1)\}}{d-2}
,
\sigma_{3}\geq\frac{2(1/2+t_{3})}{d-2}
– observe that
t_{3}\leq\frac{d-2}{2}-\frac{3}{4}
, and
1=\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}\overset{!}{\geq}\frac{d-4}{d-2}
, so this is possible.

Of course this also holds if we replace
D^{s_{1}}
with
|\nabla|^{s_{1}}
etc. – we are going to write
\tilde{D}^{s}
if it does not matter if it is
|\nabla|^{s}
or
D^{\lfloor s\rfloor}|\nabla|^{s-\lfloor s\rfloor}
.

We record the obtained above estimate

(5.4)

\begin{split}&\max_{s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}=s,s_{1}\geq 1}\|\tilde{D}^{t}\left(\tilde{D}^{s_{1}}u^{i}H_{|\nabla|}(\tilde{D}^{s_{2}}u^{j},\tilde{D}^{1+s_{3}}u^{k})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}}^{2}.\end{split}

Thus, for
t=\frac{1}{2}
if
d\geq 4
and odd, and
t=0
if
d\geq 4
and even, we arrive at

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\sum_{j}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{i},|\nabla|u^{j})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\sum_{j}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},|\nabla|u^{i})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}}^{2}\\
&+\max_{s_{2}+s_{3}=\lfloor\frac{d-2}{2}\rfloor,s_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|\sum_{j}|\nabla|^{t}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(D^{s_{2}}u^{i},|\nabla|D^{s_{3}}u^{j})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\max_{s_{2}+s_{3}=\lfloor\frac{d-2}{2}\rfloor,s_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|\sum_{j}|\nabla|^{t}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(D^{s_{2}}u^{j},|\nabla|D^{s_{3}}u^{i})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

We first treat some special cases.
We observe

(5.5)

\begin{split}&h(x)\,H_{|\nabla|}(f,g)(x)\\
=&c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{h(x)\,\left(f(x)-f(y)\right)\,\left(g(x)-g(y)\right)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy\\
=&c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\,\left(h(x)f(x)-h(y)f(y)\right)\,\left(g(x)-g(y)\right)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy\\
&+c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(h(x)-h(y)\right)\,\left(f(x)-f(y)\right)\,\left(g(x)-g(y)\right)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy\\
&-cf(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(h(x)-h(y)\right)\,\left(g(x)-g(y)\right)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy\\
=&H_{|\nabla|}(fh,g)(x)-f(x)H_{|\nabla|}(h,g)(x)+H_{|\nabla|,3}(f,g,h)(x)\\
\end{split}

where we denote the triple commutator

H_{|\nabla|,3}(f,g,h)(x):=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(f(x)-f(y)\right)\,(g(x)-g(y))\,\left(h(x)-h(y)\right)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy

We have with (
A.3
), observing that
\langle{\bf u},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}-s_{2}}{\bf u}\rangle=H_{\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}-s_{2}}}({\bf u}\cdot,{\bf u})

(5.6)

\max_{s_{2}\in[0,\frac{d-1}{2}]}\||\nabla|^{t}H_{|\nabla|}(\tilde{D}^{s_{2}}u^{i},\langle{\bf u},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}-s_{2}}{\bf u}\rangle)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Thus, with the help of (
5.5
),
Lemma

A.3
and (
5.4
) we can treat all expressions where more than
1
derivative hits one of the
u^{j}
-terms, at the expense of scalar product terms
\langle{\bf u},|\nabla|^{s}{\bf u}\rangle
which are treated in (
5.6
) – as long as
s_{3}\neq\frac{d-2}{2}
, namely we have:

(5.7)

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\sum_{j}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{i},|\nabla|u^{j})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\sum_{j}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},|\nabla|u^{i})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}}^{2}\\
&+\max_{s_{2}+s_{3}=\lfloor\frac{d-2}{2}\rfloor,s_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},s_{3}=\frac{d-2}{2}}\|\sum_{j}|\nabla|^{t}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(D^{s_{2}}u^{i},|\nabla|D^{s_{3}}u^{j})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\max_{s_{2}+s_{3}=\lfloor\frac{d-2}{2}\rfloor,s_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+};s_{2}=0}\|\sum_{j}|\nabla|^{t}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(D^{s_{2}}u^{j},|\nabla|D^{s_{3}}u^{i})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

That is we still need to estimate (the second term on the right-hand side (
5.7
) is only nonzero if
d
is even, thus
t=0
)

(5.8)

\|\sum_{j}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{i},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{j})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and

(5.9)

\|\sum_{j}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{i})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and

(5.10)

\|\sum_{j}|\nabla|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d-1}{2}}u^{i})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Estimate of (
5.8
)
:
We observe similar to (
5.5
)

\begin{split}&\left\lvert\sum_{j}u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{i},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{j})(x)\right\rvert\\
\lesssim&\left\lvert H_{|\nabla|}(u^{i},\langle{\bf u},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\rangle)(x)\right\rvert\\
&+\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left\lvert u^{j}(x)-u^{j}(y)\right\rvert\,\left\lvert u^{i}(x)-u^{i}(y)\right\rvert\,\left\lvert\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{j}(y)\right\rvert}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy\right\rvert\end{split}

We have with (
A.3
)

(5.11)

\max_{s_{2}\in[0,\frac{d-1}{2}]}\|H_{|\nabla|}(u^{i},\langle{\bf u},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\rangle)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

For the other term, pick in
[
6
, Lemma 2.11]
,
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in(0,1)
with
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}>1
,
p_{1}=\frac{2d}{2\alpha_{1}-1}
,
p_{2}=\frac{2d}{1+2(\alpha_{2}-1)}
, and
p_{3}:=\frac{2d}{d-2(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}-1)}
such that

\frac{dp_{3}}{d+(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}-1)p_{3})}=2

Observe that the last two assumptions require that
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\approx 1
.

Then from
[
6
, Lemma A.7]
we find

\begin{split}&\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left\lvert u^{j}(x)-u^{j}(y)\right\rvert\,\left\lvert u^{i}(x)-u^{i}(y)\right\rvert\,\left\lvert\tilde{D}^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{j}(y)\right\rvert}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy\right\rvert^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{1}}u^{j}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2\alpha_{1}-1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{2}}u^{i}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2(\alpha_{2}-1)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

Where the last inequality is Sobolev’s embedding (observe that
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}<1
).

This implies (
5.8
).

Estimate (
5.9
) is proven
in
Lemma

A.2
.

Lastly we need the
estimate of (
5.10
)

\|\sum_{j}|\nabla|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d-1}{2}}u^{i})\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}u^{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

This is essentially the same estimate as above, once we observe (using that
|{\bf u}|=1
)

\sum_{j}u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},\tilde{D}^{\frac{d-1}{2}}u^{i})(x)=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|{\bf u}(x)-{\bf u}(y)|^{2}\left(\tilde{D}^{\frac{d-1}{2}}u^{i}(x)-\tilde{D}^{\frac{d-1}{2}}u^{i}(y)\right)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy

This finishes the proof of the corresponding perturbative estimate for (
2.7
), and we can conclude.
∎

6.
Wellposedness: Proof of Corollary
1.2

6.1.
Short-time existence of solutions for smooth initial data

We begin with the following existence result, which can be proven by relatively standard methods

Theorem 6.1

(Existence half-wave equation)
.

Let
d\geq 3
. For any
K
suitably large, and any
\Gamma>0
there exists
T
such that the following holds:

Assume that
{\bf u_{0}}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},{\mathbb{S}}^{2})
such that

\|\nabla{\bf u_{0}}\|_{H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\Gamma.

Then there exists a solution
{\bf u}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T),{\mathbb{S}}^{2})
such that

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}{\bf u}={\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)$}\\
{\bf u}(0)={\bf u_{0}}\quad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\\
\end{cases}

Moreover we have

(6.1)

\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}((-T,T),H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}<\infty.

and

(6.2)

\|\partial_{t}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}+\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}+\|\nabla{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}<\infty.

and for any
s>1
suitably close to
1
we have

(6.3)

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{C_{t}^{0}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}<\infty\end{split}

First we solve a related equation, cf.
[
13
, (2-1)]
, without the assumption of
{\bf u}
mapping into
{\mathbb{S}}^{2}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

For any
K\in{\mathbb{N}}
and any
\Gamma>0
there exists an
T=T(\Gamma,K)>0
such that the following holds:

Assume that
{\bf u_{0}}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},{\mathbb{S}}^{2})
such that

\|d{\bf u_{0}}\|_{H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\Gamma.

Then there exists a map
{\bf u}\in C^{K}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T),\mathbb{R}^{3})
, solving

(6.4)

\begin{split}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf u}=&{\bf u}\left(|\nabla{\bf u}|^{2}-|\partial_{t}{\bf u}|^{2}\right)+\Pi_{{\bf u}_{\perp}}|\nabla|{\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u}\cdot|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\\
&+{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right).\end{split}

with initial boundary data

(6.5)

\begin{cases}{\bf u}(0)={\bf u_{0}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\\
\partial_{t}{\bf u}(0)={\bf u_{0}}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\\
\end{cases}

and we have
|{\bf u}|\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)
,

Here we denote

\Pi_{u_{\perp}}({\bf v})={\bf v}-\frac{{\bf u}}{|{\bf u}|}\langle{\bf v},\frac{{\bf u}}{|{\bf u}|}\rangle

Moreover
{\bf u}
satisfies (
6.2
), (
6.1
), and (
6.3
).

Proof.

This is a consequence of a typical fixed point argument, see, e.g.,
[
31
, p.57]
:

Set
{\bf u_{1}}:={\bf u_{0}}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}
. Clearly, assuming
K
is large enough, we have

\|{\bf u_{1}}\|_{H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|{\bf u_{1}}\|_{H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and
{\bf u_{1}}\cdot{\bf u_{0}}\equiv 0
.

We want find some
T>0
so that there exists a solution
{\bf u}\in{\bf q}+L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T),\mathbb{R}^{3})
to

\begin{cases}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf u}=H({\bf u})\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)$}\\
{\bf u}(0)={\bf u}_{0}\\
\partial_{t}{\bf u}(0)={\bf u}_{1}.\end{cases}

where

(6.6)

\begin{split}H({\bf u}):=&{\bf u}\left(|\nabla{\bf u}|^{2}-|\partial_{t}{\bf u}|^{2}\right)+\Pi_{{\bf u}_{\perp}}|\nabla|{\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u}\cdot|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\\
&+{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right).\end{split}

For
k
suitably large and
T
to be fixed later, define for
{\bf u}\in L^{2}((-T,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{3})
the norm

\|{\bf u}\|_{X}:=\sup_{t\in(-T,T)}E_{k}({\bf u},t)

where

E_{k}({\bf u},t):=\|d{\bf u}(t)\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Observe that since
d\geq 3
,
\|{\bf u}\|_{X}<\infty
implies
{\bf u}-{\bf q}(t)\in L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some constant-in-space
{\bf q}(t)
,
t\in(-T,T)
.
Moreover

\begin{split}&\|{\bf u}(t_{2})-{\bf u}(t_{1})\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
=&\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\partial_{t}{\bf u}(t)\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{t}{\bf u}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\\
\lesssim&|t_{2}-t_{1}|\|{\bf u}\|_{X}.\end{split}

For
k\gg 1
we conclude

(6.7)

\|{\bf u}(t)-{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|{\bf u}(t)-{\bf u_{0}}\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq T\|{\bf u}\|_{X}.

Also, by Sobolev embedding, if
u\in X
then
u\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T),\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

We then consider the Banach space
(X,\|\cdot\|_{X})

X:=\{{\bf u}\in L^{2}_{t}L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{3}):\quad\|{\bf u}\|_{X}<\infty\}.

And for some
\delta>0
the subset

X_{\delta}:=\{{\bf u}\in X:\quad{\bf u}(0)={\bf u_{0}},\ \partial_{t}{\bf u}(0)={\bf u_{1}},\quad\|{\bf u}\|_{X}\leq\delta\}.

which is a complete metric space.

By (
6.7
): for any
\delta
there exists
T_{0}
such that for
T<T_{0}
we have for any
{\bf u}\in X_{\delta}
that
|{\bf u}|\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)
.

We now define the linear map. Given
{\bf v}\in X_{\delta}
, set
Tv\in X
the solution

\begin{cases}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)S{\bf v}=H({\bf v})\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)$}\\
S{\bf v}(0)={\bf u_{0}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\\
\partial_{t}S{\bf v}(0)={\bf u_{1}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.\end{cases}

Observe that there is an explicit formula for
Sv
, namely we have

S{\bf v}(x,t)={\bf w}(x,t)+\int_{0}^{t}{\bf H}(x,s,t)\,ds

where

{\bf w}(x,t)=\frac{e^{{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}+e^{-{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}}{2}{\bf u_{0}}+\frac{e^{{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}-e^{-{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}}{2{\rm\bf i}}\sqrt{-\Delta}^{-1}{\bf u_{1}}

and thus

\sqrt{-\Delta}^{-1}\partial_{t}{\bf w}(x,t)=-\frac{e^{{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}-e^{-{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}}{2{\rm\bf i}}{\bf u_{0}}(x)+\frac{e^{{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}+e^{-{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}}{2}\sqrt{-\Delta}^{-1}{\bf u_{1}}(x)

and thus

\|{\bf w}\|_{X}\leq C\,E(0),

with a constant independent of
T
.

By Duhamel principle
by Minkowski (observe that the initial data for
{\bf H}
appears as the
\partial_{t}
-term, hence the improvement in derivatives)

\begin{split}\|\int_{0}^{t}{\bf H}(x,s,t)\,ds\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim&\int_{0}^{t}\|{\bf H}(x,s,t)\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,ds\\
\lesssim&\int_{0}^{t}\|{\bf H}({\bf v})\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,ds\\
\end{split}

Now for
k
suitably large we need to check that for
H
as in (
6.6
), for
{\bf v}\in X_{\delta}
(assuming that
T<T_{0}(\delta)
is small enough so that
|{\bf v}|\approx 1
so that
\Pi_{{\bf v}^{\perp}}
is well-defined and nonsingular)

(6.8)

\|H{\bf v}(t)\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim(1+E_{k}({\bf v},t)^{\sigma})\,E_{k}({\bf v},t)

The only term where this is not immediate from the algebra property of
H^{k}
for
k\gg 1
is

\begin{split}&|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right)\\
=&[|\nabla|,{\bf u}\wedge](|\nabla|{\bf u}).\end{split}

The worrisome guy would be

[|\nabla|,{\bf u}\wedge](|\nabla|\nabla^{k-1}{\bf u}).

but as a consequence of Coifman-Meyer/Kato-Poince estimates (cf.
[
17
, arxiv version (6.1)]
)

\|[|\nabla|,{\bf u}\wedge](|\nabla|\nabla^{k-1}{\bf u})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim[{\bf u}]_{{\rm Lip\,}}\|d{\bf u}\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

This establishes (
6.8
). In particular, we find

\|S{\bf v}\|\lesssim C_{0}E_{k}({\bf v},0)+T\|{\bf v}\|_{X}

So if we choose
\delta:=2C_{0}E_{k}({\bf v},0)
, and
T
suitably small so that
|{\bf v}|\approx 1
we have by taking
T_{0}(\delta)
possibly smaller

\|S{\bf v}\|\leq\delta\quad\forall{\bf v}\in X_{\delta}.

That is
S:X_{\delta}\to X_{\delta}
.

We now need to show that
S
is a contraction on
X_{\delta}
. For
{\bf w}:=S{\bf v_{1}}-S{\bf v_{2}}
we have

\begin{cases}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)\left(S{\bf v_{1}}-S{\bf v_{2}}\right)=H({\bf v_{1}})-H({\bf v_{2}})\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)$}\\
\left(S{\bf v_{1}}-S{\bf v_{2}}\right)(0)=0\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\\
\partial_{t}\left(S{\bf v_{1}}-S{\bf v_{2}}\right)(0)=0\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.\end{cases}

Now similar to the argument above we have

\|\left(S{\bf v_{1}}-S{\bf v_{2}}\right)\|_{X}\lesssim T(1+\delta^{\sigma})\,\|{\bf v_{1}}-{\bf v_{2}}\|_{X},

which implies that for
T
sufficiently small
S:X_{\delta}\to X_{\delta}
is a contraction, and thus has a unique fixed point in
X_{\delta}
.

As for the regularity of
{\bf u}
, we first observe (taking
k\gg K
, that
\partial_{tt}u\in L^{\infty}
and from this we bootstrap to the full regularity.

It remains to obtain (
6.2
), but observe that by definition of
X_{\delta}
we have (for
k\gg 1
)

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in(-T,T)}\|\nabla{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup_{t\in(-T,T)}\|\partial_{t}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\sup_{t\in(-T,T)}\|d{\bf u}\|_{H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
=&\|{\bf u}\|_{X}<\delta<\infty\end{split}

which readily implies (
6.2
).

In order to see that
{\bf u}
satisfies (
6.3
) we apply
Lemma

B.1
, and we have

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}d{\bf u}(t)\|_{C^{0}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times(-T,T)}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}{\bf u}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}{\bf u_{1}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}H({\bf u})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\\
\end{split}

Observe that the norms on the right-hand side are finite by assumption, in particular

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}H({\bf u})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\lesssim C(T)\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}H({\bf u})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\overset{\eqref{eq:KSPDEbdsHk}}{<}\infty

∎

Proposition 6.3
.

Take
{\bf u}
the solution of
Proposition

6.2
, and set

w:=\left(|{\bf u}|^{2}-1\right).

Then, assuming that
K
was chosen sufficiently large, we have

(6.9)

\|d{\bf w}\|_{L^{\infty}((-T,T),H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}<\infty.

and

(6.10)

\|{\bf w}\|_{L^{\infty}((-T,T),\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\partial_{t}{\bf w}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}+\|\nabla{\bf w}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}<\infty

and

\begin{cases}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)w=2\,w\left(|\nabla{\bf u}|^{2}-|\partial_{t}{\bf u}|^{2}\right)\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[-T,T]$}\\
w(0)=0\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\\
\partial_{t}w(0)=0\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Observe that
w\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)
and

\partial_{t}w=2\partial_{t}{\bf u}\,\cdot{\bf u}=2\partial_{t}{\bf u}\,\cdot\left({\bf u}-{\bf q}\right)+2\partial_{t}{\bf u}\cdot{\bf q}.

That is

|\partial_{t}w|+|\nabla w|\lesssim|\partial_{t}{\bf u}|

Similar we can argue for space derivatives, and obtain for all
k=1,\ldots

|\nabla^{k-1}\partial_{t}w|+|\nabla^{k}w|\lesssim\left(|\nabla^{k-1}\partial_{t}{\bf u}|+|\nabla^{k}({\bf u}-{\bf q}|\right)+\text{lower order products}

Then (
6.9
) and (
6.10
) follow from (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) and Sobolev embedding for
K
suitably large.

As for the PDE, we have as in
[
13
, p.678]
,

\begin{split}&(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(|{\bf u}|^{2}-1\right)\right)\\
=&\langle{\bf u},\partial_{tt}-\Delta{\bf u}\rangle+|\partial_{t}{\bf u}|^{2}-|\nabla{\bf u}|^{2}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:KSPDE}}{=}&\left(|{\bf u}|^{2}-1\right)\left(|\nabla{\bf u}|^{2}-|\partial_{t}{\bf u}|^{2}\right)\end{split}

This establishes the desired equation for
w:=\left(|{\bf u}|^{2}-1\right)
.

Since
|{\bf u}(0)|=1
we conclude also
w(0)=0
. Moreover,

\partial_{t}w(0)=2\langle{\bf u}(0),\partial_{t}{\bf u}(0)\rangle=0,

by the initial values for (
6.5
).
∎

Lemma 6.4

(Uniqueness)
.

Let
d\geq 3
. Assume that
w\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))
satisfies

(6.11)

\|\partial_{t}{\bf w}\|_{L^{\infty}((-T,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}+\|\nabla{\bf w}\|_{L^{\infty}((-T,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})))}<\infty.

and solves

\begin{cases}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)w=wB\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)$}\\
w(0)=0\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\\
\partial_{t}w(0)=0\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.\end{cases}

where

\sup_{t\in(-T,T)}\|B\|_{L^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty.

Then
w\equiv 0
.

Proof.

Set

E(t):=\frac{1}{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla w|^{2}+|\partial_{t}w|^{2}\right)

E(t)
is finite by (
6.11
).

Then we have

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}E(t)=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)w\,\partial_{t}w\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}w\partial_{t}w\ B\\
\leq&\|w\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|\partial_{t}w\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|B\|_{L^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{d\geq 3}{\lesssim}&\|dw\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\|B\|_{L^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\leq&E(t)\,\|B\|_{L^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

Since
E(0)=0
and
E(t)\geq 0
for all
t
we get from Gronwall’s lemma that actually
E(t)\equiv 0
. This readily implies
w\equiv 0
.
∎

As a corollary of
Lemma

6.4
and
Proposition

6.3
we obtain

Corollary 6.5
.

The solution of
Proposition

6.2
actually maps into the sphere.

Proof.

We observe that

B:=2\,\left(|\nabla{\bf u}|^{2}-|\partial_{t}{\bf u}|^{2}\right)

satisfies by (
6.1
) and (
6.2
)

\sup_{t}\|B\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty

By standard interpolation we conclude

\sup_{t}\|B\|_{L^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty.

So in view of
Proposition

6.3

w:=\left\lvert{\bf u}\right\rvert^{2}-1
satisfies the assumptions of
Lemma

6.4
, and thus
w\equiv 0
. This implies
|{\bf u}|^{2}\equiv 1
.
∎

By now we have a solution
{\bf u}
that maps into the sphere of (
6.4
). All we need in order to conclude
Theorem

6.1
is the following

Proposition 6.6
.

The solution of
Proposition

6.2
actually solves the half-wave map equation

\partial_{t}{\bf u}={\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u}\quad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)$}.

Proof.

We argue as in
[
13
, Section 6]
, although our situation is simpler since we have nice initial data.

Set

X:=\partial_{t}{\bf u}-{\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u}\in L^{\infty}((-T,T),H^{K}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

and consider (
K
being large enough)

\tilde{E}(t):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|{\bf X}(t)|^{2}<\infty.

Observe that by the initial data for
{\bf u}
we have
\tilde{E}(0)=0
.

Set

\Gamma:=\sup_{t\in(-T,T)}\|{\bf u}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\nabla|{\bf u}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla{\bf u}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}{\bf u}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\overset{\eqref{eq:KSPDEbds}}{<}\infty

We have
[
13
, p.679]

\partial_{t}{\bf X}=-{\bf X}\wedge|\nabla|{\bf u}-{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{\bf X}-{\bf u}\ \langle{\bf X},{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{\bf u}+\partial_{t}{\bf u}\rangle

Thus

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{E}(t)=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\bf X}\cdot\partial_{t}{\bf X}\\
=&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\bf X}\cdot\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{\bf X}\right)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle{\bf X},{\bf u}\rangle\langle{\bf X},{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{\bf u}+\partial_{t}{\bf u}\rangle\end{split}

Clearly,

\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle{\bf X},{\bf u}\rangle\langle{\bf X},{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{\bf u}+\partial_{t}{\bf u}\rangle\right\rvert\lesssim\Gamma\,\|{\bf X}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\Gamma\,\tilde{E}(t)

Also, we use the determinant structure (where
\epsilon_{ijk}\in\{-1,0,1\}
is the Levi-Civita tensor)

\begin{split}=&\epsilon_{ijk}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{i}\,X^{j}\,|\nabla|X^{k}\\
=&\epsilon_{ijk}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u^{i}\,X^{j}\,|\nabla|X^{k}-u^{i}\,X^{k}\,|\nabla|X^{j}\\
=&\epsilon_{ijk}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(|\nabla|\left(u^{i}\,X^{j}\right)-u^{i}\,|\nabla|X^{j}\right)\,X^{k}\\
\end{split}

and thus with Coifman-Meyer/Kato-Poince estimates (cf.
[
17
, arxiv version (6.1)]
)

\begin{split}\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\bf X}\cdot\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{\bf X}\right)\right\rvert\lesssim&\|{\bf X}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|[|\nabla|,{\bf u}]({\bf X})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|\nabla{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|X\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
\lesssim&\Gamma\|X\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.\end{split}

That is, we have shown

\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{E}(t)\lesssim\Gamma E(t),

and since
E(0)=0
we conclude from Gronwall’s lemma
E(t)=0
for all
t\in(-T,T)
. Consequently,
X\equiv 0
, which implies that
{\bf u}
solves the halfwave map.
∎

6.2.
Global existence of solutions for smooth initial data with small
H^{\frac{d}{2}}\times H^{\frac{d-2}{2}}
-norm

With the the solution
{\bf u}
of
Proposition

6.2
mapping into the sphere we can find an estimate somewhat improving (
6.1
)

Proposition 6.7
.

Assume that
{\bf u_{0}}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},{\mathbb{S}}^{2})
and
{\bf u}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(T_{1},T_{2}),{\mathbb{S}}^{2})
, solves

\begin{split}\partial_{t}{\bf u}={\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u}\quad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[T_{1},T]$}\\
{\bf u}(T_{1})={\bf u_{0}}\quad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\end{split}

such that
{\bf u}
satisfies (
6.2
), (
6.1
), and (
6.3
) hold.

Then, whenever the right-hand side is finite we have (for suitably large
K
)

(6.12)

\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}\|d{\bf u}(t)\|_{H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lesssim\|d{\bf u_{0}}\|_{H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}^{2}\,\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}\|d{\bf u}\|_{H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

In particular for any
K
there exist some
\varepsilon>0
and
C>0
(independent of
T_{1},T_{2}
) such that if

\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}<\varepsilon

then

\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}\|d{\bf u}\|_{H^{K-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leq C\|{\bf u_{0}}\|_{H^{K}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Proof.

For
s\geq 0

E_{s}(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{t}|\nabla|^{s}{\bf u}|^{2}+|\nabla|\nabla|^{s}{\bf u}|^{2}.

We have

\frac{d}{dt}E_{s}(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle|\nabla|^{s}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf u},|\nabla|^{s}\partial_{t}{\bf u}\rangle

Let

\begin{split}H({\bf u}):=&{\bf u}\left(|\nabla{\bf u}|^{2}-|\partial_{t}{\bf u}|^{2}\right)+\Pi_{{\bf u}_{\perp}}|\nabla|{\bf u}\,\langle{\bf u}\cdot|\nabla|{\bf u}\rangle\\
&+{\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}\left({\bf u}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u}\right)-{\bf u}\wedge\left({\bf u}\wedge(-\Delta){\bf u}\right).\end{split}

Then we know from the Krieger-Sire result that

\begin{cases}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta){\bf u}=H({\bf u})\quad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(T_{1},T_{2})$}\\
{\bf u}(0)={\bf u_{0}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\\
\partial_{t}{\bf u}(0)={\bf u_{0}}\wedge|\nabla|{}{\bf u_{0}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\end{cases}

If
s\geq 0
(observe that
\partial_{t}{\bf u}\cdot{\bf u}=0
),

\begin{split}&\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{t}|\nabla|^{s}{\bf u}\cdot|\nabla|^{s}({\bf u}|d{\bf u}|^{2})\right\rvert\\
\lesssim&\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle|\nabla|^{s}\partial_{t}{\bf u},{\bf u}\rangle|\nabla|^{s}|d{\bf u}|^{2}\right\rvert+\||\nabla|^{s}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\cdot\\
&\quad\cdot\max_{s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}=s,s_{1}>3/4}\||\nabla|^{s_{1}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(s-s_{1}+1)-(1-\sigma_{1})(d-1-2s)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{s_{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(s-s_{2})-(1-\sigma_{2})(d-1-2s)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\\
&\quad\cdot\||\nabla|^{s_{3}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(s-s_{3})-(1-\sigma_{3})(d-1-2s)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:GN:1v2}}{\lesssim}&\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle|\nabla|^{s}\partial_{t}{\bf u},{\bf u}\rangle|\nabla|^{s}|d{\bf u}|^{2}\right\rvert\\
&+\||\nabla|^{s}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{s}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{split}

where
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}=1
, and we needed to ensure that

\sigma_{1}\in[\frac{(s_{1}-1)_{+}}{s},1],\quad\sigma_{2}\in[\frac{s_{2}}{s},1],\quad\sigma_{3}\in[\frac{s_{3}}{s},1],

which is possible since

\frac{(s_{1}-1)_{+}}{s}+\frac{s_{2}}{s}+\frac{s_{3}}{s}=\frac{s+(s_{1}-1)_{-}-s_{1}}{s}<1.

For the other term, by the same argument, using that
|{\bf u}|\equiv 1
we also get

\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle|\nabla|^{s}\partial_{t}{\bf u},{\bf u}\rangle|\nabla|^{s}|d{\bf u}|^{2}\right\rvert\lesssim\||\nabla|^{s}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

So we have

\frac{d}{dt}E_{s}(t)\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,E_{s}(t).

Integrating this implies for any
\tilde{T}\in[0,T]

E_{s}(\tilde{T})\leq E_{s}(0)\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\max_{t\in[0,\tilde{T}]}E_{s}(t)

taking the supremum of
\tilde{T}
on the left-hand side, we conclude

\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}E_{s}(t)\lesssim\|d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}E_{s}(t).

Recall that this holds for any
s\geq 0
.
We have established
(
6.12
) and can conclude.

∎

As a consequence of
Theorem

6.1
,
Proposition

6.7
and the uniqueness result of
[
6
]
, and the a priori estimates
Theorem

1.1
we obtain global existence.

Proof of
Corollary

1.2
.

Take
K
sufficiently large,
\varepsilon
from
Proposition

6.7
.
We first assume that additionally

(6.13)

\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{H^{K-1}}<\infty

Take
T
from
Theorem

6.1
for
\Gamma:=C\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{H^{K-1}}
, where
C>1
is from
Proposition

6.7
.

Then there exists a uniform
T_{1}:=T
such that we can solve

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}{\bf u_{1}}={\bf u_{1}}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u_{1}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(0,T)$}\\
{\bf u_{1}}(0)={\bf u_{0}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\end{cases}

For
k\geq 2
set
T_{k}:=(k-\frac{3}{2})T
and
u_{k}:=u_{k-1}\Big|_{T_{k}}
, which solves

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}{\bf u_{k}}={\bf u_{k}}\wedge(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf u_{k}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(T_{k}-T,T_{k}+T)$}\\
{\bf u_{k}}(T_{k})={\bf u_{k-1}}\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\end{cases}

Since by
Proposition

6.7
we know
{\bf u_{1}}(T_{1}-T)
satisfies uniform bounds, namely

\|\nabla u_{1}(T_{2})\|_{H^{K-1}}\leq C\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{H^{K-1}}\leq\Gamma

we
u_{2}
exist for the same
T
as before, by
Theorem

6.1
. By uniqueness proven in
[
6
]
(observe that we have (
6.1
)) we conclude that
u_{2}\equiv u_{1}
where the domain of definition overlap. In particular we have from
Proposition

6.7

\|\nabla u_{2}(T_{3})\|_{H^{K-1}}\leq C\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{H^{K-1}}\leq\Gamma

We repeat this argument inductively to obtain
u_{k}
, and we have that
u_{k}=u_{k-1}
where their domains of definitions overlap.

Thus we may set
u(x,t):=u_{k}(x,t)
whenever
t\in(T_{k}-T,T_{k}+T)
we have found a solution in
[0,\infty)
(and similarly we argue to obtain a solution in
(-\infty,0]
.

So we have established global existence under the additional assumption (
6.13
) (and from the a priori estimates
Theorem

1.1
we obtain

(6.14)

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{C_{t}^{0}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-\infty,\infty))}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-\infty,\infty))}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{split}

which in particular implies (
1.6
))

In case that
{\bf u_{0}}
does not satisfy (
6.13
). Since
\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\varepsilon

by density of smooth maps in
\dot{H}^{\frac{d}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},{\mathbb{S}}^{2})
, we find
{\bf u_{0}}
by
{\bf u_{0;\ell}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},{\mathbb{S}}^{2})
which still satisfies

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0;\ell}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<2\varepsilon

Since we have the support condition that
{\rm supp\,}{\bf u_{0}}-{\bf q}
is compact we can also obtain that
{\bf u_{0;\ell}}\in H^{K}
for any
K
.

We then find a global solution
{\bf u}_{\ell}
a global solution with initial data
{\bf u_{0;\ell}}
with the estimates (
6.14
). We pass to the limit and by lower semicontinuity of the involved norms we obtain

(6.15)

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-\infty,\infty))}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}d{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-\infty,\infty))}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{split}

Again this implies (
1.6
). As mentioned before the uniqueness result was proven in
[
6
]
.
∎

Appendix A
Leibniz Rule and Sobolev-Gagliardo-Nirenberg embeddings

In this section we formulate several estimates for the Leibnizrule for operators like
|\nabla|^{\alpha}
as well as Gagliardo-Nirenberg-type inequalities. These estimates are known at least to experts and we make no claim of originality.

A.1.
Leibniz rule

We begin by the basic Leibniz rule, which can be proven, e.g.
[
17
, Theorem 7.1.]
, see also the presentation in
[
10
, Theorem 3.4.1]
.

Here, and henceforth the Leibniz-rule operator is denoted by

H_{|\nabla|^{\alpha}}(f,g)=|\nabla|^{\alpha}(fg)-(|\nabla|^{\alpha}f)\,g-f\,|\nabla|^{\alpha}g

A simple, yet extremely useful observation by many people is that for
\alpha\in(0,2)

(A.1)

H_{|\nabla|^{\alpha}}(f,g)(x)=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(f(x)-f(y)\right)\,\left(g(x)-g(y)\right)}{|x-y|^{d+\alpha}}\,dy.

Lemma A.1

(Leibniz rule estimate)
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\beta\in(0,2)
. Pick any
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that
\alpha+\beta-\gamma\in(0,1)
, and
p,p_{1},p_{2}\in(1,\infty)
such that

\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}.

Then

(A.2)

\||\nabla|^{\alpha}H_{|\nabla|^{\beta}}(f,g)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\gamma}f\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha+\beta-\gamma}g\|_{L^{p_{2}}}.

As a consequence of repeated Leibniz-type arguments we also have

Lemma A.2
.

Assume that
{\bf u}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
such that
|{\bf u}|_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}^{2}\equiv 1
a.e. in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then,

\left\|\sum_{j}u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},F)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

Proof.

We are going to use a well-known facts, such as

\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{|x-y|^{\alpha}}\lesssim\mathcal{M}||\nabla|^{\alpha}f|(x)+\mathcal{M}||\nabla|^{\alpha}f|(y)\quad\text{a.e. $x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$}

which can be found e.g. in
[
29
, Proposition 6.6.]
; Here
\mathcal{M}
is the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function.
Also, we observe that since
|{\bf u}(x)|^{2}=|{\bf u}(y)|^{2}=1

{\bf u}(x)\cdot({\bf u}(x)-{\bf u}(x))=\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert{\bf u}(x)-{\bf u}(x)\right\rvert^{2}.

Then, by duality, for some
\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1
, and for any choice of
\alpha\in(1/2,1)
such that
d-(2\alpha-1)2>0
,

\begin{split}&\|\sum_{j}u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},F)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{j}u^{j}H_{|\nabla|}(u^{j},F)\,G\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:HDsalpharep}}{=}&\frac{c}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|{\bf u}(x)-{\bf u}(y)|^{2}\,\left(F(x)-F(y)\right)G(x)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dydx\\
\lesssim&\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}F(x)G(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|{\bf u}(x)-{\bf u}(y)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dydx\right\rvert\\
&+\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}G(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|{\bf u}(x)-{\bf u}(y)|^{2}F(y)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dydx\right\rvert\\
=&\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}F(x)G(x)H_{|\nabla|}({\bf u}\cdot,{\bf u})\right\rvert\\
&+\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}G(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|{\bf u}(x)-{\bf u}(y)|^{2}F(y)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dydx\right\rvert\\
\lesssim&\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|H_{|\nabla|}({\bf u}\cdot,{\bf u})\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
