&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert G(x)\right\rvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(\left(\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}(x)\right)^{2}+\left(\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}(y)\right)^{2}\right)\left\lvert F(y)\right\rvert}{|x-y|^{d+1-2\alpha}}\,dydx\\
\overset{\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ERFS22}{}{}, (A.7)]}}}{\lesssim}&\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert G(x)\right\rvert\,\left(\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}(x)\right)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x-y|^{2\alpha-1-d}\left\lvert F(y)\right\rvert\,dydx\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}(y)\right)^{2}\,\left\lvert F(y)\right\rvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x-y|^{2\alpha-1-d}\left\lvert G(x)\right\rvert dxdy\\
=&\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert G(x)\right\rvert\,\left(\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}(x)\right)^{2}\,|\nabla|^{-(2\alpha-1)}\left\lvert F\right\rvert(x)dx\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}(y)\right)^{2}\,\left\lvert F(y)\right\rvert|\nabla|^{-(2\alpha-1)}\left\lvert G\right\rvert(y)dy\\
\lesssim&\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
&+\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|\left(\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}\right)^{2}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2(2\alpha-1)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{-(2\alpha-1)}\left\lvert F\right\rvert\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-(2\alpha-1)2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\|\left(\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}\right)^{2}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2(2\alpha-1)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{-(2\alpha-1)}\left\lvert G\right\rvert\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-(2\alpha-1)2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
&+\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2\alpha-1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\|\mathcal{M}|\nabla|^{\alpha}{\bf u}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2\alpha-1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\\
&+\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

We can conclude.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Set

H_{|\nabla|,3}(f,g,h)(x):=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(f(x)-f(y)\right)\,(g(x)-g(y))\,\left(h(x)-h(y)\right)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy

Denote by
\tilde{D}^{s}
either
|\nabla|^{s}
or
D^{\lfloor s\rfloor}|\nabla|^{s-\lfloor s\rfloor}
. Then if
d\geq 4
is even and
t=0
or if
d\geq 5
and
t=\frac{1}{2}
, assuming that
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\in[0,\frac{d}{2}-t)
and

s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+t=\frac{d}{2}

Then

\||\nabla|^{t}H_{|\nabla|,3}(\tilde{D}^{s_{1}}u^{i},\tilde{D}^{s_{2}}u^{j},\tilde{D}^{s_{3}}u^{k})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

Proof.

If
d
is even, and thus
t=0
, from
[
6
, Lemma A.6.]
, for
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in[0,1]
such that
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}=1
we have

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{t}H_{|\nabla|,3}(\tilde{D}^{s_{1}}u^{i},\tilde{D}^{s_{2}}u^{j},\tilde{D}^{s_{3}}u^{k})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{s_{1}+\alpha_{1}}u^{i}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2(s_{1}+\alpha_{1}-1)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{s_{2}+\alpha_{2}}u^{j}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2(s_{2}+\alpha_{2}-1)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{s_{3}+\alpha_{3}}u^{k}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{3}+2(s_{3}+\alpha_{3}-1)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:GN:1}}{\lesssim}&\||\nabla|{\bf u}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}{\bf u}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

For this to be correct, we need some assumptions.

For the application of
[
6
, Lemma A.6.]
observe that
[
6
, (A.4)]
is naturally satisfied since
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}<\frac{d}{2}
.

For the application of (
A.3
) we need
s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1<\frac{d-2}{2}
which is easy to establish since
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=1
,
s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}=\frac{d}{2}
, and each
s_{i}<\frac{d}{2}
(and
d\geq 4
).

So the conditions we need to ensure are

\sigma_{j}\geq\max\{\frac{2(s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1)}{d-2},0\},\quad j=1,2,3.

If all
s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1>0
for
j=1,2,3
then we see that

\sum_{j=1}^{3}\max\{\frac{2(s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1)}{d-2},0\}=\frac{d-4}{d-2}<1

so we can easily choose
\sigma_{j}
with the requirements above.

If (say)
s_{1}+\alpha_{1}-1<0
and
s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1>0
,
j=2,3
then we can assume that
\alpha_{1}
is as close to
1
as possible, which means

s_{2}+\alpha_{2}-1+s_{3}+\alpha_{3}-1\approx\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{2(s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1)}{d-2}=\frac{d-4}{d-2}<1.

Then we choose

\sigma_{j}\geq\max\{\frac{2(s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1)}{d-2},0\},\quad j=2,3

such that
\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}<1
, and pick
\sigma_{3}=1-\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{3}
. Clearly any permutation of indices does not affect the argument.

The last case is that (say)
s_{1}+\alpha_{1}-1<0
and
s_{2}+\alpha_{2}-1<0
. In this case we can assume (by choice of
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
as large as possible)

s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1>-\frac{1}{4},

so that

1+2(s_{j}+\alpha_{j}-1)>0\quad j=1,2

Then we simply choose

\sigma_{3}\in(\frac{2(s_{3}+\alpha_{3}-1)}{d-2},1)

and for any choice of
\sigma_{1}
,
\sigma_{2}
such that
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}
we found an admissible tuple for the application above of (
A.3
).

The same principle can be applied for
t=\frac{1}{2}
and
d\geq 5
odd. In this case we need to replace
[
6
, Lemma A.6.]
to show that

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{\frac{1}{2}}H_{|\nabla|,3}(f,g,h)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{1}+\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{L^{p_{1;1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{2}}g\|_{L^{p_{2;1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{3}}h\|_{L^{p_{3;1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{1}}f\|_{L^{p_{1;2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{2}+\frac{1}{2}}g\|_{L^{p_{2;2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{3}}h\|_{L^{p_{3;2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{1}}f\|_{L^{p_{1;3}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{2}}g\|_{L^{p_{2;3}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha_{3}+\frac{1}{2}}h\|_{L^{p_{3;3}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

The proof is left to the reader, it follows from the arguments
[
6
, Lemma A.6.]
combined with the estimates in
[
23
, Section 3]
after representing

\begin{split}H_{|\nabla|,3}(f,g,h)(x):=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}K(x,y,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})|\nabla|^{\alpha_{1}}f(z_{1})\,|\nabla|^{\alpha_{2}}g(z)\,|\nabla|^{\alpha_{3}}h(z)\,dz_{1}dz_{2}dz_{3},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}&K(x,y,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(|x-z_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}-d}-|y-z_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}-d}\right)\,\left(|x-z_{2}|^{\alpha_{2}-d}-|y-z_{2}|^{\alpha_{2}-d}\right)\,\left(|x-z_{3}|^{\alpha_{3}-d}-|y-z_{3}|^{\alpha_{3}-d}\right)}{|x-y|^{d+1}}\,dy\end{split}

and estimating with the arguments of
[
23
, Section 3]

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{K(x_{1},y,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})-K(x,y,z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})}{|x_{1}-x|^{d+\frac{1}{2}}}\,dx_{1}

We leave the details to the reader.
∎

A.2.
Gagliardo-Nirenberg type inequalities

We also will use versions of the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, which we collect in the following lemma

Lemma A.4

(Higher order Gagliardo-Nirenberg)
.

Throughout this lemma we assume
d\geq 3
.

(1)

For
s\in[0,\frac{d-2}{2}]
and
\sigma\in[\frac{2s}{d-2},1]

we have

(A.3)

\||\nabla|^{s}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}.

The same holds for
s<0
as long as
\sigma\in[0,1]
and we have
\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s}\in(1,\infty)
.

(2)

For
s\geq 0
,
t\in[0,s]
and
\sigma\in[\frac{t}{s},1]

(A.4)

\||\nabla|^{t}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(s-t)-(1-\sigma)(d-1-2s)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{s}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}.

(3)

For any
\sigma\in[\frac{2\alpha}{d-2},1]
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
with
\frac{2d}{2+2\alpha+\sigma}\in(1,\infty)
we have

(A.5)

\||\nabla|^{\alpha}(fg)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2+2\alpha+\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\max_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}\in\{f,g\}}\|\tilde{g}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2-\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}

(4)

We have

(A.6)

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left({Q}f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\left(1+\|Q\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\,\max_{\tilde{f}\in\in\{f,g,h,{|\nabla|\tilde{Q}}\}}\left(\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\,\max_{\tilde{g}\in\{f,g,h,{|\nabla|\tilde{Q}}\}}\|\tilde{g}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{{2}}\end{split}

(5)

Recall the commutator notation

[T,f](g)=T(fg)-fT(g).

Let
s\leq\frac{d-2}{2}
,
\sigma\in[0,1]
such that
1+2s-\sigma>0
. If
s\geq 1
assume additionally
(1-\sigma)\in{(}\frac{2(s-1)}{d-2},1]
(observe the strict inequality!)

Then

(A.7)

\||\nabla|^{s}\left([\mathscr{R},|\nabla|^{-1}f](g)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s-\sigma}}}\lesssim\max_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}\in\{f,g\}}\|\tilde{g}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1+\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}

Proof.

(1)

We prove (
A.3
).

If
s<0
then from the Sobolev inequality,

\||\nabla|^{s}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Then the claim follows from the case
s=0
(observe that the assumptions of
\sigma
are still satisfied).

So, from now on we assume that
s\geq 0
.

For
\sigma=1
(
A.3
) is simply Sobolev inequality. For
\sigma=0
, the assumption
\sigma\geq\frac{2s}{d-2}
implies that
s\leq 0
, i.e.
s=0
, and then (
A.3
) is trivial.

So from now on assume
\sigma\neq 0,1
and
s\geq 0
.

If
\sigma\geq\frac{2s}{d-2}
then
t
defined by

\sigma=\frac{2t}{d-2}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad t=\frac{d-2}{2}\sigma

satisfies
t\in[s,\frac{d-2}{2})
.

Moreover, from Sobolev embedding

\||\nabla|^{s}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{t}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2t}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

By this argument, otherwise replacing
s
with
t
, we can reduce the claim to the case

\sigma=\frac{2s}{d-2}.

Then,

s=(1-\sigma)0+\sigma\frac{d-2}{2},

and

\frac{1+\sigma+2s}{2d}=\left(1-\sigma\right)\frac{1}{2d}+\sigma\frac{1}{2}.

Consequently, classical Gagliardo-Nirenberg (see, e.g.,
[
2
, Lemma 3.1.]
) implies

\||\nabla|^{s}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma+2s}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}.

(2)

The proof of (
A.4
) is very similar to (
A.3
).

For
\sigma=1
it is Sobolev embedding. For
\sigma=0
we must have
t=s=0
and then (
A.4
) is trivial. So from now on
\sigma\neq 0,1
.

If we set
\tilde{t}
defined by

\frac{\tilde{t}}{s}:=\sigma\quad\Leftrightarrow\tilde{t}=s\sigma\geq t

then from Sobolev embedding we have

\||\nabla|^{t}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(s-t)-(1-\sigma)(d-1-2s)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\tilde{t}}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(s-\tilde{t})-(1-\sigma)(d-1-2s)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

Now we have

\tilde{t}=(1-\sigma)0+\sigma s

and

\frac{d-2(s-\tilde{t})-(1-\sigma)(d-1-2s)}{2d}=(1-\sigma)\frac{1}{2d}+\sigma\frac{1}{2}

so we have

\||\nabla|^{\tilde{t}}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2(s-\tilde{t})-(1-\sigma)(d-1-2s)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{s}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma},

from the usual Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality.

(3)

Proof of (
A.5
): The case
\alpha<0
can be reduced to
\alpha=0
by means of Sobolev embedding

\||\nabla|^{-\alpha}(fg)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2-2\alpha+\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|fg\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2+\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

So assume from now on
\sigma\in[\frac{2\alpha}{d-2},1]
and
\alpha\in[0,\frac{d}{2}-1]
.

For each
\beta\in[0,\alpha]
we pick
\sigma_{1}\in[\frac{2\beta}{d-2},1]
,
\sigma_{2}\in[\frac{2(\alpha-\beta)}{d-2},1]
with
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}=\sigma
.

Then, from the fractional Leibniz rule and (
A.3
),

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{\alpha}(fg)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2+2\alpha+\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim&\max_{\beta\in[0,\alpha]}\||\nabla|^{\beta}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2\beta}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\alpha-\beta}g\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2(\alpha-\beta)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|f\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{1}}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-1}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{1}}\ \|g\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{2}}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-1}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{2}}\\
=&\max_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}\in\{f,g\}}\|\tilde{g}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2-\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma}\end{split}

Here and throughout the paper we mean by an abuse of notation with
\max_{\beta\in[0,\alpha]}
the maximum over finitely many specific
\beta\in\{0,\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{[a]},\alpha\}\subset[0,\alpha]
(that can be computed from the Leibniz rule).

(4)

In preparation of the proof of (
A.6
) we first prove for any
\alpha\geq 0
such that
\frac{2d}{d-2\alpha}\in(1,\infty)
,

(A.8)

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}-\alpha}\left(f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\max_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}\in\{f,g,h\}}\|\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}}\,\|\tilde{g}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

By Sobolev inequality

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}-\alpha}\left(f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left(f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\end{split}

so in order to prove (
A.8
) we may assume w.l.o.g.
\alpha=0
.

We have from the Leibniz rule

(A.9)

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left(f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \|H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\max_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}=\frac{d-2}{2}}\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{1}}f\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{2}}g\|_{L^{p_{2}}}\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{3}}h\|_{L^{p_{3}}}\end{split}

Here, in the max we have
\gamma_{1}\in[0,\frac{d}{2}-1)
,
\gamma_{2}>-1
,
\gamma_{3}>-1
. Indeed, by the fractional Leibniz rule we can ensure for any fixed
\varepsilon_{0}>0

\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}>-\varepsilon_{0}

Moreover
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
can be chosen differently for each tuple
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})
.

The first term in (
A.9
) can be estimated by
[
6
, Lemma A.8, (A.7)]

\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}}\|H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|h\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

For the second term in (
A.9
) we first consider the case where
\gamma_{3}\leq 0
and
\gamma_{2}\leq 0
. Observe that
\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}>-1
so the following makes sense. We estimate the second line of (
A.9
) by

\begin{split}\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{1}}f\|_{L^{\frac{d2}{2+2\gamma_{1}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\gamma_{2}}g\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\gamma_{2}2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{3}}h\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\gamma_{3}2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}\leq 0}{\lesssim}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|g\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|h\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\end{split}

Now we assume that
\gamma_{3}\leq 0
and
\gamma_{2}>0
. Then we estimate the second line of (
A.9
) by

\begin{split}\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{1}}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2\gamma_{1}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\gamma_{2}}g\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2\gamma_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{3}}h\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\gamma_{3}2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\gamma_{3}<0}{\lesssim}&\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{1}}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2\gamma_{1}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\gamma_{2}}g\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2\gamma_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|h\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\end{split}

where we choose
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}=1
so that

\frac{1+\sigma_{1}+2\gamma_{1}}{2d}+\frac{1+\sigma_{2}+2\gamma_{2}}{2d}=\frac{1}{2}

We need to ensure that

\sigma_{i}\geq\frac{2\gamma_{i}}{d-2}\quad i=1,2

Observe that since
\gamma_{3}
is negative, but very close to zero,

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{2\gamma_{i}}{d-2}=\frac{d-4}{d-2}+\frac{-2\gamma_{3}}{d-2}\overset{|\gamma_{3}|\ll 1}{<}1=\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}

so the conditions on
\sigma_{i}
can be satisfied, and we have

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{1}}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2\gamma_{1}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \||\nabla|^{\gamma_{2}}g\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2\gamma_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|h\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\|f\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{1}}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\sigma_{1}}\ \|g\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{2}}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\sigma_{2}}\ \|h\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim&\max_{\tilde{f}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\max_{\tilde{g}\in\{f,g,h\}}\|\tilde{g}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\end{split}

The case
\gamma_{3}\leq 0
and
\gamma_{2}>0
is almost verbatim to the previous one.

Now we assume
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}\geq 0
. In this case we estimate the second line of (
A.9
) by

\begin{split}\lesssim&\max_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}=\frac{d-2}{2}}\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{1}}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2\gamma_{1}}}}\,\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{2}}g\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2\gamma_{2}}}}\,\||\nabla|^{\gamma_{3}}h\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{3}+2\gamma_{3}}}}\\
\end{split}

where and we choose
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}=1
. In order to conclude with the help of (
A.3
) we set

\sigma_{i}:=\frac{2\gamma_{i}}{d-2},\quad i=1,2,3.

We have established (
A.8
).

We now prove (
A.6
)

Using the fractional Leibniz rule we have

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left({Q}f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|Q\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\left(f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-1}Q\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|f\|_{L^{d}}\|H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&+\max_{\alpha\in(0,\frac{d}{2}-1)}\||\nabla|^{\alpha}Q\|_{L^{\frac{d}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-1-\alpha}\left(f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2\alpha}}}.\end{split}

The first line is estimated by (
A.8
) (
\alpha=0
). The second line is estimated by Sobolev embedding and
[
6
, Lemma A.8, (A.7)]
,

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-1}Q\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|f\|_{L^{d}}\|H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}Q\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\ \|f\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\|g\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

For the third line we use again (
A.8
) and Sobolev inequality and conclude

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\alpha}Q\|_{L^{\frac{d}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-1-\alpha}\left(f\,H_{|\nabla|}(|\nabla|^{-1}g,|\nabla|^{-1}h)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2\alpha}}}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}Q\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\max_{\tilde{f}\in\{f,g,h\}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\max_{\tilde{g}\in\{f,g,h\}}\|\tilde{g}\|_{L^{(2d,2)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{{2}}\end{split}

(5)

Now we prove (
A.7
):

The case
s<0
can be reduced to the case
s=0
, since by assumption,
\frac{2d}{1+2s-\sigma}\in(1,\infty)
, and by Sobolev embedding,

\||\nabla|^{s}\left([\mathscr{R},|\nabla|^{-1}f](g)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s-\sigma}}}\lesssim\|\left([\mathscr{R},|\nabla|^{-1}f](g)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1-\sigma}}}.

So assume now
s\in[0,1)
,
\sigma\in[0,1]
, and either
\sigma<1
or
s>0
. From commutator estimates,
[
10
, (2.3.3)]
, for
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in[0,1]
,
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}=1-\sigma
, and
for any
\beta\in(0,1)
.

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{s}\left([\mathscr{R},|\nabla|^{-1}f](g)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s-\sigma}}}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\beta-1}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2(\beta-1)}}}\||\nabla|^{s-\beta}g\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2(s-\beta)}}}\\
\end{split}

Since
s<1
we can choose
s<\beta<1
, and then with (
A.3
),

\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{1}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\sigma_{1}}\|g\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{2}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\sigma_{2}}.

If
s=1
we can make the same argument, only that we have
\beta<1=s
. However we can make
s-\beta
arbitrarily small. So if
1-\sigma>0
then we can take
\beta
suitably close to
1
so that
\sigma_{2}
additionally satisfies
\sigma_{2}\geq\frac{2(s-\beta)}{d-2}
, and then the same argument as above is true.

We argue similarly for
s>1
,
\sigma\in[0,1]
and
(1-\sigma)\in{(}\frac{2(s-1)}{d-2},1]
. From commutator estimates,
[
10
, (2.3.3)]
, for any
\delta>0

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{s}\left([\mathscr{R},|\nabla|^{-1}f](g)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+2s-\sigma}}}\\
=&\||\nabla|^{s}\left([\mathscr{R},|\nabla|^{-1}f](g)\right)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2+2(s-1)+1-\sigma}}}\\
\lesssim&\max_{t\in[-\delta,s-1]}\||\nabla|^{t}f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{1}+2t}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\||\nabla|^{s-1-t}g\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{1+\sigma_{2}+2(s-1-t)}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:GN:1}}{\lesssim}&\|f\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{1}}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-1}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{1}}\ \|g\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma_{2}}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}-1}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\sigma_{2}}\\
=&\max_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}\in\{f,g\}}\||\nabla|\tilde{g}\|_{L^{2d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1+\sigma}\ \||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\sigma}.\end{split}

For this to make sense we must choose
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}=1-\sigma
,
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in[0,1]
such that additionally

\sigma_{1}\in[\frac{2t}{d-2},1],\quad\sigma_{2}\in[\frac{2(s-1-t)}{d-2},1].

If
t\geq 0
this is no problem, by the assumptions, since

(1-\sigma)\in{(}\frac{2(s-1)}{d-2},1]

For the case
t<0
, we need to assume that
\delta\ll 1
so that

(1-\sigma)>\frac{2(s-1+\delta)}{d-2}.

Then pick
\sigma_{1}=0
and we observe

\sigma_{2}=(1-\sigma)\in[\frac{2(s-1-t)}{d-2},1].

∎

Appendix B
Strichartz estimates

The following estimate is a consequence of a careful inspection of the arguments in
[
32
, p.566]
. See also
[
11
, Corollary 1.3]
. It follows from Duhamel principle and the semigroup-representation for the homogeneous wave equation

(B.1)

\begin{cases}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)v=0\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,T]$}\\
v=f\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0\}$}\\
\partial_{t}v=g\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0\}$}.\end{cases}

Namely we have

v(x,t)=\frac{e^{{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}+e^{-{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}}{2}f(x)+\frac{e^{{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}-e^{-{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}}{2{\rm\bf i}}\sqrt{-\Delta}^{-1}g(x)

and thus

\sqrt{-\Delta}^{-1}\partial_{t}v(x,t)=-\frac{e^{{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}{-}e^{-{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}}{2{{\rm\bf i}}}f(x)+\frac{e^{{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}{+}e^{-{\rm\bf i}t\sqrt{-\Delta}}}{{2}}\sqrt{-\Delta}^{-1}g(x)

Lemma B.1
.

Let
d\geq 4
. Assume for
T>0

(B.2)

\begin{cases}(\partial_{tt}-\Delta)u=h\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[-T,T]$}\\
u=f\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0\}$}\\
\partial_{t}u=g\quad&\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0\}$}\\
\end{cases}

then for all
s\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{d^{2}-4d+1}{2(d-1)}+1]
,

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}du(t)\|_{C^{0}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times(-T,T)}+\||\nabla|^{s}u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}h\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}.\end{split}

as well as

\begin{split}\||\nabla|^{s-1}du(t)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\|{|\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}h}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}.\end{split}

In particular,

\begin{split}&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}du(t)\|_{C^{0}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times(-T,T)}+\||\nabla|^{s-1}u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{(\frac{2d}{2s-1},2)}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T)}\\
\lesssim&\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-2}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&+\||\nabla|^{\frac{d-4}{2}}h\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(-T,T))}.\end{split}
